**Not closed by this section.** *Carried from section 16.22, updated.* The checks section 16.22 listed as not built are built, except those below: #20172 built the oracle's `ill-typed` in `v2-fault`, the order of R115 (b), the table check in the `v2-binding` reader (R113), R117 and the `Int` model parameter in its kit reader; #20170 and #20169 built `ill-typed` for a fault case's table, R117 and the `Int` model parameter in the two lanes, R108 with R118 in the TypeScript lane, and R116 in the Python lane's library. **Still open:** whether `Int` as a model result needs a line in [D23] (R112); the table check of R108 in `v2-domain` when it gets a `types` field (R114; the oracle's side comes with S11d); R107 (c) and R116 in the two lanes' corpus readers (S6b, S7b); **what the oracle's kit reader does with R107 (a), (b) and (f)** (a repeated binder name, the shape of a `reference` body, a device name in the claim: it checks none, and (c) and (d) do not apply to it; (e), the grammar of an `error` case name, is built, because `check_corpus_and_row` reads `types` through the table check); **whether the generator checks a `types` object it reads** (not looked at); two questions of the S7a review, **what a `tuple` descriptor against a scalar type is in `v2-domain`**, whose code set has no level code (R116 (c) is the same question for `list`), and **what a consumer does with a chunk file that agrees with its row and not with the enumeration**; and, of section 16.20's items, **whether an `omit_example` line may omit an example that R99 refuses** and **what a lane does with a `pre` rewrite whose value is not a value of the parameter's declared type**. *Added by this erratum.* **The owner's call on a code for a malformed fault row and for an undefined level name in `fault.requires`** (R126 reads both as kit errors). **The orders R119 to R122 do not pin**, which the tools disagree on and no vector can observe (R25): among `unknown-tag`, `depth` and `model-order` in `v2-fault`; a model's signature against `level-missing` and a tree defect in `v2-ir`; an `env` type refusal against a tree defect. **An error type of a v0 document named after a v1 type** (`error U16 = ...`): the oracle writes the row and every consumer refuses it (R123). **Whether a corpus reader must refuse an unknown tag in the fault row of a not-live control** (R119 (c): the `v2-fault` order puts `level-missing` first, unlike the suite-level rule of R69 (d)). **The oracle's `ir.list`**: its evaluator refuses `List` types (R124 (a)), so a `v2-ir` case that reaches one has no oracle derivation; it belongs to the slice that gives the oracle that level and is not asked for here. **The checks that are not built yet,** all queued above: the oracle's three codes, `env` code, schema check, op-signature order, R58 set, unreached-model rule and chunk-corpus reader; the Python lane's op-row order, `env` code and `model-order` order, and S6b's row-field order; the TypeScript lane's R58 names and `types: null` in `v2-value` (#20174, open).

## Appendix A. `ir.core` tags

| Tag | Form | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `lit` | `["lit", true]`, `["lit", {"type":T,"value":V}]` | `V` per section 2.3; Bytes literals are hex. The written type of a bytes `lit` is always `Bytes` and a Bool literal is always the bare form: a `lit` whose written type is `Bytes<N>`, or the object form with type `Bool`, is `ill-typed`. A bytes `lit` has the static type `Bytes<len>` (section 16.16 R51; section 16.18 R73) |
| `var` | `["var", name]` | a binder or model parameter |
| `subject_op` | `["subject_op", name, [args][, [widths]]]` | claim bodies only. The fourth element is the list of static widths: one JSON integer in `0..=4096` per entry of the op's `width_params`, in order, present exactly when the op has `width_params`. A consumer substitutes them into the op's signature before typing the arguments. A missing or extra entry, an entry that is not such an integer, or a list on an op without `width_params`, is `ill-typed` (section 16.18 R70) |
| `model_call` | `["model_call", name, [args]]` | |
| `len` | `["len", e]` | `Nat` |
| `ok`, `err` | `["ok", e]`, `["err", "CASE"]` | `err` takes the case name. A bare `ok(e)` has an undetermined error type and a bare `err` an undetermined payload; each unifies with any `Result` whose other component matches. The case of an `err` must be declared by an error type in `types`. A kit case never has a result type with an undetermined component (section 16.15 R41) |
| `eq ne lt gt le ge` | two operands | same-type, or Nat/Int widening |
| `add sub mul` | two operands | section 3 |
| `and or implies` | two Bool operands | short-circuit |
| `not` | one Bool operand | |
| `if` | `["if", c, t, e]` | |
| `concat take drop` | on `Bytes` | total: `take`/`drop` clamp, a negative count is `0`. The count is a `Nat` or an `Int`; a fixed-width count is `ill-typed` (section 16.18 R74). On a list operand all three are `ir.list` (section 16.16 R57) |
| `is_err` | one `Result` operand | Bool (no source form: only fault templates write it) |
| `neg` | one operand | wraps (section 3); the v0 generator refuses it, the v2 generator does not. The signed minimum literal (`-128i8`) lowers to `neg` of a `lit` holding `-2^(w-1)`; `neg` wraps it to itself and counts in `wraps` |

`neg`, `concat`, `take`, `drop` and `is_err` are the five v2 additions to the 21 tags v1 emits today: `lit var subject_op model_call len ok err eq ne lt gt le ge add sub mul and or not implies if`. The list was reconciled with the generator source in review (section 15.2, item 8).


### A.1 `ir.fixed` and `ir.tuple` tags [D24, D20; implemented in S10a to S10c-2 and S10t-1, S10t-2]

One tag per source built-in, same spelling, positional operands; a static type argument is the canonical type name as a JSON string in position 1. The shapes of the two fault rows are kept byte for byte (`["bit_and", ["var", p], ["lit", {"type":"U64","value":"9223372036854775807"}]]`). `T` is a fixed-width type of width `w`, `S` a signed one, `U` an unsigned one.

| Tag | JSON form | Operand types | Result | Semantics (VER04j 2.3, 3.2, 3.3) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `bit_and bit_or bit_xor` | `[tag, a, b]` | `T, T` | `T` | per bit of the `w`-bit two's-complement pattern, re-read in `T` |
| `bit_not` | `["bit_not", a]` | `T` | `T` | flip `w` bits (`bit_not(0i8) = -1i8`) |
| `shl` | `["shl", a, n]` | `T, Nat` | `T` | zero fill; `n >= w` gives 0 |
| `shr` | `["shr", a, n]` | `T, Nat` | `T` | **logical** on the pattern, then re-read (`shr(-1i8,1) = 127i8`); `n >= w` gives 0 |
| `sar` | `["sar", a, n]` | `S, Nat` | `S` | sign fill; `n >= w` gives `-1` if `a < 0` else `0` |
| `rotl rotr` | `[tag, a, n]` | `T, Nat` | `T` | rotate by `n mod w` |
| `popcount clz ctz` | `[tag, a]` | `T` | `Nat` | on the `w`-bit pattern; `clz`/`ctz` of 0 is `w` |
| `bit` | `["bit", a, i]` | `T, Nat` | `Bool` | bit `i`, LSB 0; `false` for `i >= w` |
| `widen` | `["widen", "U32", e]` | fixed, range inside the target | target | value preserved |
| `wrap` | `["wrap", "I8", e]` | fixed, `Int` or `Nat` | target | value mod `2^w`, re-read in the target |
| `try` | `["try", "U8", e]` | fixed, `Int` or `Nat` | `Option<target>` | `Some` iff in range |
| `to_int` `to_nat` | `[tag, e]` | fixed / `U` | `Int` / `Nat` | mathematical value |
| `max_value min_value` | `[tag, "I64"]` | none | the type | not folded to `lit` (section 10, unoptimised lowering) |
| `checked_add checked_sub checked_mul` | `[tag, a, b]` | `T, T` | `Option<T>` | `None` iff the exact result leaves `T` |
| `div rem` | `[tag, a, b]` | `U, U` or `Int`/`Nat` | `Option<U>` / `Option<Int>` | `None` iff `b == 0`; `Int`/`Nat`: floor, `rem` has the divisor's sign. **Signed fixed-width operands are refused** (use `div_trunc`/`rem_trunc`) and two `Nat` operands give `Option<Int>` |
| `div_trunc rem_trunc` | `[tag, a, b]` | `S, S` | `Option<S>` | toward zero; `None` iff `b == 0` or (`a == MIN` and `b == -1`) |
| `imin imax` | `[tag, a, b]` | `Int`/`Nat` | `Int` | exact |
| `be_bytes le_bytes` | `[tag, e]` | `T` | `Bytes<w/8>` | two's-complement bytes |
| `from_be from_le` | `[tag, "U32", e]` | `Bytes<w/8>` | target | a runtime length other than `w/8` is a corpus error, never a truncation |
| `at` | `["at", b, i]` | `Bytes`/`Bytes<N>`, `Nat` or `Int` | `Option<U8>` | `None` for `i < 0` or `i >= len` (the list form is reserved with `ir.list`, [D46]) |
| `unwrap_or` | `["unwrap_or", o, d]` | `Option<T>, T` | `T` | both operands evaluated (no short circuit; cost is the sum) |

Notes. Shift counts are exact `Nat` (bounded only by the `2^127` window): an evaluator compares `n >= w` **before** any host shift, and `rotl`/`rotr` reduce `n mod w` first. `wraps` counts `add sub mul neg` only [D31]. Every tag costs `1 +` the sum of its operands (section 4.2 rule 6); the `ceil(w/8)` work units of VER04j 3.5 belong to the Rust harness meter, not to `ir_cost`. Typing is inferred, as for `add`: a consumer types operands from `lit`, `var` and model signatures, and a mismatch (mixed widths, `sar` on an unsigned type, a count that is not `Nat`) is a corpus error. **`div`/`rem` typing is normative [D24, R7]**: it is the typing of the grammar checker, `code/packages/rust/warrant-grammar/src/checker.rs`, the `"div" | "rem"` arm (line 3231 in the tree this erratum is based on; `Option<Int>` for `Int`/`Nat` operands, `Option<U>` for matching unsigned fixed operands, a signed pair refused). **`Option<T>` is an intermediate type only [D23]: its single consumer is `unwrap_or`, as the first operand and directly** (section 16.10 D23); from S11a that direct form is the *intermediate use*, which needs `ir.fixed` only, and every other `Option` is the real type of `ir.data` (Appendix A.2, [D48]). **The static width of a `from_be`/`from_le` operand** (the `Bytes<w/8>` it must have) is computed by the grammar checker's rule, `checker.rs` (`Literal::Bytes`, the `concat` arm and `Type::common_type`): a bytes literal is `Bytes<len>`, a `concat` of two statically sized operands is `Bytes<sum>`, an `if` whose two branches have the same static width keeps it (otherwise the branches unify to plain `Bytes`), and `take`/`drop` give plain `Bytes`. A consumer must implement the same rule (S10b included), so that `from_le<U32>(concat(t, b"000000"))` with `t: Bytes<1>` is accepted by every tool and a plain `Bytes` operand is refused by every tool. Fixed-width types and literals, `+ - *`, unary `-` and comparisons on them are `ir.core` (section 3.2) and unchanged.

`ir.tuple` (provisional until S10t-1 reads it, section 16.10 R2):

| Tag | JSON form | Result |
|---|---|---|
| tuple literal | `["tuple", [e1, .., en]]`, 2 to 8 operands | `(T1,..,Tn)` |
| `project` | `["project", e, i]`, `i` a 0-based JSON integer | `Ti` |

`ir.tuple` `match` on `Result` (the plan's shape, section 16.10 R2; provisional until S10t-1): `["match", s, [[pat, arm], ..]]` with `s` of type `Result<X,E>` and patterns `["ok", pat]`, `["err", "CASE"]`, `["err_any"]`, `["tuple", [pats]]`, `["bind", name]`, `["wild"]`. The **patterns** are tried in order and **exactly one arm body is evaluated**, the arm of the first pattern that matches (section 4.2 rule 2). Author refinements (section 16.10 R2; the plan is silent), each a corpus error with the generator code `ir` (oracle `corpus`), refused by both tools in S10t-1/2: a `match` that can fall through (not exhaustive over `ok`/`err` cases); **an arm that no value can reach** (every value it matches is matched by an earlier arm, decided by the usual usefulness check over the patterns); the **same `bind` name twice in one pattern**; and a `bind` name that **shadows** a binder, a parameter, `result` or an outer `bind` (there is no shadowing). Cost: `1 +` cost(scrutinee) `+` the maximum over the arms (section 4.2 rule 6). A `match` whose scrutinee is a bare tuple, variant, record or `Option` is `ir.data`; the same JSON, a different level (the scrutinee's static type decides). A lane that implements `ir.tuple` without `ir.data` therefore types the scrutinee before it accepts a `match`, and refuses as `level-missing` one whose scrutinee is not a `Result` or that holds a data pattern (section 16.18 R76). All arm bodies of a `match` must have one type; a mismatch is a typing error (code `type` at source, `ir` in the IR typer), like mismatched `if` branches, not a `corpus` error (author refinement, S10t-1 review).

### A.2 `ir.data` tags [D47, D48, D60; provisional until S11a's committed dump]

The shapes follow Appendix A.1's conventions: one tag per source construct, positional operands, a static type argument as the canonical type name (a JSON string) in position 1. **Provisional (section 16.14 [D47], the R2 precedent):** S11a's committed lowering dump (`tests/fixtures/data-forms-v1/` in `python/warrant-oracle`) is the first reader of this appendix; where it must differ, this appendix and the `field-zero` block of section 8.2 are corrected by erratum before S11b reproduces the dump. While it is provisional nothing outside the two tools reads it: no committed corpus, conformance vector or lane runner names an A.2 tag before S11d, which follows S11c-1 (section 13). `N` is a record name, `V` a variant name, `C` a constructor name; names, field lists and constructor lists are those of `types` (section 2.2).

| Tag | JSON form | Operand types | Result | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `some` | `["some", e]` | any value type `T` | `Option<T>` | source `Some(e)` |
| `none` | `["none", "T"]` | none; `T` is the payload's canonical type name (section 2.1; `Int` is allowed here as in a `lit`) | `Option<T>` | source `None`; the lowerer writes the type the position expects (the [D24] convention), so a consumer types the node without context. Not `["none"]`: an untyped node cannot be typed bottom-up |
| `ctor` | `["ctor", "Name", [e1, .., en]]` | one operand per declared field, in declaration order, each assignable to its field's type | the record `N` when `Name` is a record's name; the variant `V` that declares the constructor `Name` otherwise | source `Name(e1, .., en)` and the bare name of a payload-free constructor, which is `["ctor", "Add", []]`. Records, constructors and the callable names share one namespace (VER04j section 4.2), so the name alone fixes the type |
| `field` | `["field", e, i]` | `e` a **record**; `i` a 0-based JSON integer below the field count | the declared type of field `i` | source `r.name`; the name is resolved to its index at lowering and stays in `types`. A variant has no `field` (use `match`); a tuple uses `project` |

`match` on any scrutinee (the same JSON as Appendix A.1, `["match", s, [[pat, arm], ..]]`; the level is decided by the scrutinee's static type and the patterns, below). The patterns of Appendix A.1 (`ok`, `err`, `err_any`, `tuple`, `bind`, `wild`) are joined by four **data patterns**:

| Pattern | JSON form | Matches a value of type | When |
|---|---|---|---|
| `some` | `["some", pat]` | `Option<T>` | the value is `Some(x)` and `pat` matches `x` |
| `none` | `["none"]` (no type: the scrutinee has one) | `Option<T>` | the value is `None` |
| `ctor` | `["ctor", "Name", [pat1, .., patn]]` | the record or variant that declares `Name` | the value's constructor is `Name` and every `pati` matches field `i`; `n` is the declared field count (`[]` for a payload-free constructor) |
| `bool` | `["bool", true]`, `["bool", false]` | `Bool` | the value is that literal (VER04j has no integer or bytes literal pattern) |

**Levels.** `some`, `none`, `ctor` and `field` are `ir.data` tags. A `match` contributes **`ir.tuple` when its scrutinee's static type is `Result<X,E>` and `ir.data` otherwise** (section 4.1, [D35]), **and `ir.data` as well whenever any of its patterns, at any depth, is a data pattern** (`Ok(true) -> ..` on a `Result<Bool,E>` requires both). The level is type-directed: a consumer decides it from the scrutinee's static type and the patterns, never from the JSON shape, which is the same at both levels (section 16.18 R76). The **type rule** of section 16.10 R9, as amended: `ir.data` is added whenever the static type of any sub-expression, binder, op or model parameter or result, or example value is or contains a record, a variant or an `Option`, **except** the static type of a producer node in the intermediate use (`unwrap_or(producer(..), d)`, [D23] as amended): that `Option` adds nothing, so every `requires` computed before this appendix existed is unchanged. A type that is or contains a list is not counted at all: the document is refused (`ir.list`, section 4.1).

**Typing.** Inferred, as everywhere: operands from `lit`, `var`, model and op signatures and the static type arguments. `ctor` arguments are checked per field with the grammar checker's assignability for constructor arguments (VER04j section 4.4: `Nat` to `Int` and `Bytes<N>` to `Bytes` per argument, and **no** subtyping through a tuple, a record or a variant; an `Option` and a `Result` lift by their payload, [D60]; `code/packages/rust/warrant-grammar/src/checker.rs` is normative, as it is for `div`/`rem`, and S11a reads it before it writes the dump). A pattern is typed against the type of the value it meets: `ok`/`err`/`err_any` need a `Result`, `some`/`none` an `Option`, `ctor` the record or variant that declares the name, `tuple` a tuple of that arity, `bool` a `Bool`; `bind` and `wild` take any type, and a `bind` gives its name the type of the value. All arm bodies have one type (a mismatch is a typing error, as in Appendix A.1). `eq` and `ne` on two values of one record, variant or `Option` type are structural (section 4.2 rule 5): the same constructor and equal fields, `None == None`, `Some(x) == Some(y)` iff `x == y`. The ordering tags are not defined on these types (VER04j section 4.3) and are a typing error there. `unwrap_or` takes any `Option<T>` first operand (section 16.14 R29); the other `ir.fixed` built-ins are unchanged. **`some` and `none` (section 16.14 R39 as corrected by [D60]; section 16.16 R51, R52):** `["some", e]` has exactly the type `Option<type(e)>`, and **`Option` follows the checker**: an `Option<S>` flows to `Option<T>` when `S` flows to `T`, and two `Option` types unify by their payloads, as `Result` does (`Option<Bytes<4>>` flows to `Option<Bytes>` and `Option<Nat>` to `Option<Int>`; `if c then Some(n) else Some(i)` with `n: Nat` and `i: Int` is an `Option<Int>`; `checker.rs` is normative here too). Tuples, records and variants do not lift (`Option<(Nat,Nat)>` does not flow to `Option<(Int,Int)>`). The lift writes no node and changes no value. A source `None` takes its payload type from the **expected type** of its position and from nothing else, never from a sibling branch, arm or operand: one whose position has no expected `Option` type (`None == None`, `match None with ..`, `unwrap_or(if c then at(b, i) else None, d)`) is a typing error (code `type`), never a guess; R52 lists the positions. **Projection at source:** from S11a `e.i` on a value that is not a tuple and `e.name` on a value that is not a record are typing errors (code `type`), no longer the level `ir.data`.

**No pattern binds an error value [D55].** `["err", "CASE"]` and `["err_any"]` are the only error patterns. A source arm `Err(e) -> body` lowers to `["err_any"]` when `body` does not mention `e` (the name is still declared, for the duplicate and shadowing rules) and is refused with the feature name `err-value` when it does (section 4.1).

**Corpus errors** (generator code `ir`, oracle `corpus`), refused by both tools from S11a/S11b: those of Appendix A.1, over the larger pattern set (a `match` that can fall through; an arm no value can reach, by the usual usefulness check over `Bool`, `Option`, `Result`, variants, tuples and records; the same `bind` name twice in one pattern; a `bind` that shadows a binder, a parameter, `result` or an outer `bind`, or that is named like a declared model, op, error type or constructor (a record's name included, since it is its type's one constructor) or a reserved built-in of the document's version (the checker's W0203 set; the oracle since CV2-F8, #19720)), and: a `ctor` or `ctor` pattern whose name is not declared or whose operand count is not the declared field count; a `field` whose index is not below the field count or whose operand is not a record; a `none` whose type argument is not a canonical type name; a pattern whose form does not fit the type of the value it meets. The last four items are errors of the IR reader (oracle code `ir`). At source the same mistakes are typing errors (oracle `type`, generator `check`), as for mismatched arm types (section 16.16 R55). A `match` has at most 256 arms and a pattern nests at most 8 deep (VER04j section 4.3; over either is the tools' `limit`). Section 16.14 R35 orders the refusals.

**Evaluation and cost.** The patterns are tried in order and exactly one arm body is evaluated (section 4.2 rule 2); a pattern has no cost of its own. `some`, `ctor` and `field` cost `1 +` the sum of their operands, `none` costs 1, and `match` costs `1 +` cost(scrutinee) `+` the maximum over the arms (section 4.2 rule 6), whatever the scrutinee. `wraps` counts none of them [D31]. Values are those of section 2.3 (`{"none":true}`, `{"some":v}`, a record as the array of its fields, `{"ctor":..,"fields":[..]}`), streamed as section 2.4 says (`40`/`41`; `64` for a record, exactly as a tuple of its fields; `65` with the constructor name) and written to TFV as section 2.5 says. A suite whose every type is in the v1 type set of section 1.2 keeps the v1 stream header (`Result<Option<U8>,E>` is in it; a record or a variant is not).
